33|## 1. Literal general-k NF, GRX and accepted baseline
34|
35|Write kappa:=kappa_hat for the NORMAL-FORM parameter, not original kappa(w). NF preserves avoidance,
36|length,F and the rotation multiset from w to u only; no inverse avoidance-preservation is assumed. Its cyclic
37|representation is R_1 S_1 ... R_k S_k, S_i=sigma_(y_i)^(2h_i+1), h_i>=0; r_i=|R_i|>=0, and every r_i=0
38|has y_(i-1)!=y_i. Set
39|  H=sum h_i=(F-k)/2=p-1-kappa, F=k+2H, R=sum r_i=2p+2kappa-k.
40|These are verified GENERAL-k NF identities, not p+1 specialisations. In particular kappa<=p-1. GRX all-plus
41|gives kappa>=0 and ZP_kappa on the odd rotation lift Y(x)=(1,x) in C_2 x C_p=C_(2p). Every zero-sum of odd
42|terms has even size. NF also gives1<=k<=o_F(w),k=o_F(w) mod2, and

46|  T(z)=sum_(z_i=+1)(y_(i-1)-y_i) mod p, X^z=union_i z_i X_i,
47|where X_i is the actual rotation-value occurrence multiset in run i. Since every block is odd, GRX has
48|C_max=F-(k-Phi)=2H+Phi. Therefore an identity2p-selection exists EXACTLY when some z and EVEN n satisfy
49|  max(0,2kappa+2-Phi)<=n<=min(R,2p-Phi),   T(z) in Sigma_n(X^z).                    (G)
50|An empty size interval gives no test. Sigma_0={0}. This is freshly derived from GENERAL GRX by n=2p-C,
51|not a p+1 lift. For a nonempty proper cyclic interval P of runs, z=-1 on P has Phi2 and

68|## 2. Odd-block pair padding and a cyclic color lemma
69|
70|In any all-odd NF, selecting zero or one representative reflection from each block leaves h_i disjoint
71|same-value pairs available in that block, independently of its selected parity. Any integer0..H is a sum
72|of choices0..h_i. Thus an identity skeleton selection of EVEN length L with
73|  2kappa+2=2p-2H<=L<=2p
74|can be padded with(2p-L)/2 pairs to identity2p in u. Conversely deleting selected even pairs from a2p
75|identity selection gives such a skeleton selection. This is selection bookkeeping, not a new word mutation.
76|The skeleton has length R+k=2p+2kappa and a DIFFERENT forbidden-length interval; it is not a p+1 normal form.
77|Cyclic cuts conjugate products and preserve identity in the original index order.
78|
79|In the critical kappa1 family, any pure representative-reflection identity of length4or6 is therefore
80|forbidden (6<=2p for p>=5). We need this elementary COLOR fact:
81|
82|A cyclic sequence of p+2 reflection values over F_p, each value occurring at most twice, cannot avoid all
83|pure reflection identities4and6. Indeed two doubled values A,B must INTERLACE: AABB and ABBA already have
84|product identity. If three values double, pairwise interlacing gives ABCABC up to relabelling/cyclic cut.
85|Its product is(sigma_A sigma_B sigma_C)^2=1, since an odd product of reflections is a reflection. Hence at
86|most two values double. p+2 positions over p values then force exactly two doubles A,B and all p values
87|present, in cyclic order ABAB on those four positions. The value C=2A-B is distinct from A,B since p is odd,
88|so it is present once. The two A-to-A arcs each contain a B. Choose the arc NOT containing C: the resulting
89|cyclic selection A,B,A,C has product rho_(2A-B-C)=1, forbidden. This proves the COLOR fact.               []
90|
91|## 3. Critical kappa1 when Y has a zero-sum pair
92|
93|Let the pair be rotation values x,-x. If x=0, the k>p block values contain two equal values; their interval
94|pattern has Phi2,T0, and the two zero rotations are a signed sum0 for either sign. At kappa1,n2 satisfies(G),
95|giving identity2p, contradiction.
96|
97|If x!=0 and a reflection value occurred at least three times, take three consecutive occurrences of that
98|value around the cycle. Their three intervening run arcs partition the rotation occurrences; one contains
99|neither marked rotation. Its endpoints have equal value, so its Phi2 pattern has T0 and the two marked
100|rotations lie in the SAME sign zone. Their signed sum is0. Again n2 is admissible in(G), contradiction.
101|Thus every reflection value occurs at most twice. The COLOR fact now supplies a pure4or6 identity, which
102|pair padding turns into identity2p in u. This closes the entire zero-sum-pair case, with no S(p) premise. []
103|
104|## 4. Critical kappa1 when Y is zero-sum-free
105|
106|Use the ACCEPTED classical S(p) statement: a zero-sum-free sequence of p odd terms of C_(2p) has an odd
107|generator g with least positive ODD coefficients a_i summing W<=2p-1. This is imported at secondary-restatement
108|trust (Xia-Yuan through Peng-Sun, primary proof unread), NOT claimed proved or upgraded here. Write
109|u=#coefficient1 terms, s=#larger-coefficient terms, so u+s=p. The word 'one' here refers to COEFFICIENT1,
110|not all group generators. Put E=W-p<=p-1. If s>=2, every larger coefficient b satisfies
111|  b<=E-2(s-1)+1<=p-2s+2<=p-s=u.
112|If s=1, b is odd and b<=p. The only case with b>u=p-1 is b=p, namely
113|  Y=g^(p-1)(pg).                                                                (X)
114|If s=0 all coefficients are1.
115|
116|Outside(X), EVERY bipartition of Y into two nonempty parts admits an EVEN signed zero-sum of size2..p-1.
117|If coefficient-one terms occur in both parts, choose one on each side. Otherwise all u such terms are on
118|one side. Choose a term b from the other side and b coefficient-one terms from the first; their signed
119|coefficients cancel exactly, length b+1 even. If s>=2, b+1<=u+1<=p-1; if s=1 outside(X), odd b<=p-2 gives
120|the same bound. The s=0 case necessarily split ones. All choices use actual distinct occurrences.
121|
122|Suppose a reflection value appeared in two DIFFERENT maximal reflection runs of u. Both cyclic arcs between
123|those blocks contain rotations, so they give a bipartition of Y into two nonempty parts. The equal-boundary
124|Phi2 pattern has T0. The preceding cancellation gives a signed even rotation selection of size2..p-1,
125|which lies inside(G)'s Phi2 interval[2,min(p,2p-2)]. Contradiction. Therefore the color palettes of the p
126|maximal reflection runs are disjoint. Each palette is nonempty; k=p+2>p and the genuine empty rule force
127|at least one run to have at least two different block values. The palettes would require at least p+1 colors
128|in F_p, impossible. This closes the nonexceptional zero-sum-free case without imposing rotation rigidity. []
129|
130|For(X), g=(1,c) has c!=0, so rotations are rho_c^(p-1),rho_0. Choose a sign pattern with FOUR changes;
131|it exists on the cycle k=p+2>=7 (take two nonadjacent runs as the minus set). Let A,B count the c-letters
132|with positive/negative signs, A+B=p-1. Every integer d in[-B,A] is obtained using |d| signed c-letters;
133|include the one zero rotation exactly when |d| is odd. This gives an EVEN size0..p-1 and signed sum dc.
134|There are p consecutive integers d, so every residue of F_p is obtained. At Phi4,kappa1, all these even
135|sizes lie in(G): lower0, upper min(p,2p-4)>=p-1. Some selection has signed sum T(z), contradiction.
136|This is a FRESH general-GRX four-change argument; old ARC0's different zero multiplicities are not imported. []

143|## 5. The top diagnostic: proved necessary structure, NOT exclusion
144|
145|Now p>=7,k=p+2,kappa=p-1. The verified NF formulas give H0, every block SINGLETON, F=k,R=3p-4. The full
146|residual is the specialization of Sec.6, not merely the tests below. Here(G) has the EXACT rotation size
147|  n=2p-Phi
148|for every sign pattern (all such sizes are available, since0<=Phi<=p+1 and R>=2p). The all-plus odd Y has
149|ZP_(p-1). Accepted CT at n_CT=p-4>=1, |Y|-n_CT=2p, therefore gives
150|  sum_a min(p-4,v_a(Y))<=2p-6.                                                   (CT-top)
151|This is CT on the ORIGINAL all-plus Y only, not on arbitrary signed multisets without their own ZP.
152|
153|For EVERY pattern Phi>=4 a signed value has multiplicity at least2p-2. Here is the complete new-range
154|calculation, not an application of the old k=p+1 Lemma P. A multiset of size N,maximal multiplicity mu has
155|q disjoint unequal-value pairs if2q<=N and q<=N-mu: cap multiplicities at q, whose total is>=2q (the largest
156|keeps q if necessary and the other caps retain at least q); take2q items with each value consecutive and
157|pair positions i,i+q. Equality would fill q+1 equal positions, impossible. This proves the generic pair lemma.
158|
159|If signed mu<=2p-3, apply it to R=3p-4 with q=p-1. Phi>=4 gives n=2p-Phi in[p-1,2p-4]. Outside the q
160|pairs lie p-2 items; choose n-(p-1) in[0,p-3] fillers. Taking one item from each unequal pair gives sums
161|base+sum_(i in I)d_i, with p-1 nonzero differences d_i. Iterated classical Cauchy-Davenport fills all F_p.
162|Some actual n-rotation selection sums to T(z), violating(G). Thus mu>=2p-2 as asserted.
163|
164|The heavy value is unique since2(2p-2)>R. Its ORIGINAL unsigned value class contains at least2p-2 letters.
165|These classes coincide over all Phi>=4 patterns: two different unsigned classes are disjoint and would
166|exceed R. Denote the common class{a,-a}. It is nonzero: otherwise at least2p-2 original rotations are zero;
167|k>p gives two equal block values, whose Phi2,T0 pattern admits precisely n=2p-2 zero rotations, contradiction.
168|Hence at most p-2 rotations lie outside one NONZERO unsigned pair, and each Phi>=4 pattern has a signed
169|value in that pair of multiplicity>=2p-2. This is NEAR-PAIR structure, NOT a claim all rotations lie in a pair.
170|At Phi2, n=2p-2 would require p-1 fillers after the same q pairs, but only p-2 are available: the argument
171|fails by ONE filler. It is not a no-go theorem. No full top-family exclusion or feasible tuple is claimed.

46|**Theorem P: fresh full-proof check**
47|
48|The group law gives
49|\[
50|(a_1,e_1)\cdots(a_s,e_s)
51|=
52|\left(\sum_j\Bigl(\prod_{\ell<j}e_\ell\Bigr)a_j,\ \prod_j e_j\right).
53|\]
54|Thus the prefix reflection parity supplies precisely the signs used in P Step 4. The reflection contribution telescopes as printed, and a selected rotation pair contributes \(x_\pi(m_\pi-u_t)\) on the Boolean box. Consequently \(V\) has degree **at most one**, including the possibility \(V=0\).
55|
56|The size polynomial agrees with the actual selected length on Boolean points and has degree **at most two**. Therefore
57|\[
58|M=(F-1)+\sum_t\lfloor n_t/2\rfloor\ge3p-2,
59|\qquad
60|\deg Q\le3(p-1)=3p-3<M.
61|\]
62|The signed box lemma is proved within P: every monomial of degree below \(M\) omits a variable, whose flip cancels its two contributions. Fermat’s theorem makes \(Q\) the indicator of the two required congruences on the Boolean box.
63|
64|The origin contributes \(+1\), so cancellation forces another solution. Injectivity and the unique-empty-selection argument make its selection nonempty. Its length is even, divisible by the odd prime \(p\), and below \(4p\); hence it is exactly \(2p\). Its product is the identity.
65|
66|For original order, write the input word \(AB\) and its cyclic cut \(BA\). The same selected positions have products
67|\[
68|P_BP_A \quad\text{and}\quad
69|P_AP_B=P_B^{-1}(P_BP_A)P_B.
70|\]
71|Identity therefore survives restoration of the original increasing-index order, even when a constructed pair crosses that original boundary.
72|
73|Both P errata are necessary readings, and neither invalidates an HK step. No general Chevalley–Warning theorem, prime-power extension or finite computation is needed: the supplied signed-box argument is sufficient.
74|
75|P’s LF3–5 computational-checking and “not yet independently reviewed” language remains the immutable historical header. The supplied **04:22:45** acceptance records a later verification closure at its own date and scope. It does not backdate two-family verification. P’s composite-threshold remarks and conditional prime-power discussion supply no premise here.
76|
77|**General NF, GRX and every HK use of P**
78|
79|For the actual all-odd NF,
80|\[
81|F=k+2H,\qquad H=p-1-\widehat\kappa,\qquad
82|R=2p+2\widehat\kappa-k.
83|\]
84|The local NF paragraph supplies these at general \(k\); the later restricted few-odd theorem is not being extended. \(H\ge0\) gives \(\widehat\kappa\le p-1\). If \(\widehat\kappa<0\), then \(H\ge p\), and all-plus GRX permits \(2p\) reflections with zero rotations, contradicting avoidance.
85|
86|For every sign pattern,
87|\[
88|\delta=k-\Phi,\qquad C_{\max}=2H+\Phi.
89|\]
90|Substitution of \(n=2p-C\) gives exactly
91|\[
92|\max(0,2\widehat\kappa+2-\Phi)
93|\le n\le
94|\min(R,2p-\Phi),\qquad n\ \text{even}.
95|\]
96|Global sign reversal changes both the signed sum and \(T\) by a minus sign, so arbitrary patterns are legitimate. Empty gaps, empty size intervals and \(\Sigma_0=\{0\}\) are handled correctly. The interval-boundary formula \(T=y_{j'}-y_j\) matches the supplied group convention.
97|
98|Every P invocation has an actual input \(u\) of length \(4p-2\), an odd prime, and \(F\ge k\ge p+2\). Its reflection-to-reflection rotation gaps are the \(r_i\), together with internal zero gaps inside the constant reflection blocks. Hence
99|\[
100|P_{\rm gap}=\sum_i\lfloor r_i/2\rfloor,
101|\qquad
102|F+P_{\rm gap}\le3p-2,
103|\]
104|and therefore
105|\[
106|P_{\rm gap}\le p+2\widehat\kappa-k,\qquad
107|k\le p+2\widehat\kappa.
108|\]
109|
110|This validates HK LF54–59, the critical reduction LF61–65, the run structure used at LF122–128, and residual (R1) at LF182–184. The larger-\(k\) conclusion in LF138–140 and baseline attribution at LF207 have the same valid basis. **No rotation-value multiplicity is substituted for a gap length.**
111|
112|Here P’s restored “original order” is the order of its input word \(u\). Every subsequent selection is built in \(u\) and contradicts \(u\)’s avoidance. Nothing is inversely transported through normalization to \(w\).
113|
114|**The complete \(\widehat\kappa=1\) argument**
115|
116|P already excludes \(k\ge p+3\). At \(k=p+2\),
117|\[
118|H=p-2,\quad F=3p-2,\quad R=p,\quad P_{\rm gap}=0.
119|\]
120|Thus every \(r_i\) is zero or one, exactly two are empty, and there are exactly \(p\) maximal cyclic reflection runs. A zero-valued rotation remains a separating letter.
121|
122|The rotation dichotomy is exhaustive because every nonempty zero-sum of odd terms has even length, while all even lengths at least four are excluded by all-plus GRX. Any surviving nonempty zero-sum must therefore be a pair.
123|
124|Padding retains every \(h_i\). Whether a representative is selected or not, block \(i\) can supply any additional number of pairs from zero through \(h_i\). Adding these pairs preserves the ordered product. Conversely, reducing each selected block count to its parity removes at most \(H\) pairs. At \(\widehat\kappa=1\), pure representative-reflection identities of lengths four and six are consequently forbidden.
125|
126|The COLOR argument is sound. Two doubled labels must interlace; three pairwise interlacing doubled labels yield \(ABCABC\), an identity. With at most two doubles, \(p+2\) positions force exactly two doubles and all \(p\) labels present. The distinct label \(C=2A-B\) then supplies the cyclic identity \(ABAC\).
127|
128|In the zero-sum-pair branch, two zero rotations work with any signs. For a nonzero pair, three occurrences **among block labels** divide the rotation occurrences into three arcs; one misses both marked letters. Equal boundary labels give \(T=0\), and both marked rotations receive the same sign. In either case, \(n=2\) is admissible. COLOR finishes the remaining case. There is no bound on raw reflection-letter multiplicities.
129|
130|For the zero-sum-free branch, S(p) supplies positive coefficients relative to a generator. Parity makes every coefficient odd. With worker erratum’s notation,
131|\[
132|u_{\rm one}+s=p,\qquad E=W-p\le p-1,\qquad E\ge2s.
133|\]
134|For \(s\ge2\), every larger coefficient satisfies
135|\[
136|b\le p-2s+2\le p-s=u_{\rm one}.
137|\]
138|For \(s=1\), the sole exception to \(b\le u_{\rm one}\) is \(b=p\), namely \(g^{p-1}(pg)\).
139|
140|Outside that exception, every nonempty rotation bipartition has an even signed cancellation of size \(2\) through \(p-1\): either take coefficient-one occurrences from both sides, or cancel one coefficient \(b\) against \(b\) distinct coefficient-one occurrences on the other side. The signed cancellation is in the actual \(\mathbb F_p\) rotation values. No occurrence is reused.
141|
142|A repeated label in two different maximal reflection runs would then violate the equal-boundary \(\Phi=2,T=0\) test. Their palettes must be disjoint. There are \(p\) runs, and \(p+2\) blocks force one run to contain different adjacent block labels. That requires at least \(p+1\) colors, a contradiction.
143|
144|For the literal exception, the rotations are \(c^{p-1},0\), with \(c\ne0\). A four-change pattern exists without requiring all rotation runs to be nonempty. With \(A+B=p-1\), every integer \(d\in[-B,A]\) is realized using \(|d|\) appropriately signed \(c\)-letters; the zero letter corrects parity when necessary. The resulting even sizes are at most \(p-1\), and the \(p\) consecutive integers give every residue \(dc\). All these sizes are admissible at \(\Phi=4,\widehat\kappa=1\). This is a fresh GRX argument, not an ARC0 import.
145|
146|S(p) supplies the coefficient representation in the zero-sum-free argument. Once the literal exceptional rotations are given, their fresh GRX exclusion requires no additional S(p) step.
147|
148|The separate \(p=5\) statement also checks: NF has its supplied \(p\ge5\) interface; GRX and P apply; padding permits length six; four changes exist at \(k=7\); and the S(p) overlay explicitly includes \(n=10\) at secondary-restatement trust. No \(p=5\) VAC extension or computational lift is used.

150|**Top diagnostic and HIGH-K-RES**
151|
152|At \(k=p+2,\widehat\kappa=p-1\), \(H=0\), all blocks are singletons and \(R=3p-4\). Since \(k\) is odd, \(0\le\Phi\le p+1\), and GRX fixes
153|\[
154|n=2p-\Phi.
155|\]
156|All these sizes are available.
157|
158|CT applies to the **all-plus** odd sequence with \(n_{\rm CT}=p-4\), because its complement has length exactly \(2p\). This gives CT-top. It supplies no automatic CT hypothesis for arbitrary signed multisets.
159|
160|For a pattern with \(\Phi\ge4\), suppose its signed maximum multiplicity were at most \(2p-3\). The proved generic pairing lemma supplies \(p-1\) disjoint unequal pairs. The required size satisfies
161|\[
162|p-1\le n\le2p-4.
163|\]
164|There are \(p-2\) unused occurrences, sufficient for the required zero through \(p-3\) fillers. Choosing one occurrence per pair and applying Cauchy–Davenport to their \(p-1\) nonzero differences reaches every residue, contradicting GRX.
165|
166|Thus every such pattern has a unique signed value of multiplicity at least \(2p-2\). Its original unsigned class contains that many rotation occurrences. Two different unsigned classes cannot both fit within \(R=3p-4\), so the class is common across the patterns. It cannot be zero: repeated block labels would give a \(\Phi=2,T=0\) pattern admitting exactly \(2p-2\) zero rotations.
167|
168|This proves the stated **common nonzero near-pair** conclusion, with at most \(p-2\) rotations outside it. At \(\Phi=2\), the same construction requires \(p-1\) fillers but has only \(p-2\). That is a failure of this argument, not a no-go theorem, full rigidity or exclusion.

33|## 1. Theorem P: statement, domain, trust, order, degrees and every HK call
34|
35|**Statement and domain.** The verdict (LF5) restates proof LF10–28:
36|- p is any odd prime;
37|- the word has N≤4p−1 and F≥1;
38|- n_t are the cyclic rotation-gap lengths between consecutive reflections;
39|- if F+Σ⌊n_t/2⌋≥3p−1, the word has an identity 2p-subword in increasing original order.
40|
41|Nothing in the acceptance narrows this statement.
42|
43|**Proof re-derived by hand (LF30–93).**
44|- **Steps 1–3.** M=(F−1)+Σ⌊n_t/2⌋≥3p−2. The map from parameters to selections is injective, because u_{t+1}=u_t⊕[f_t selected] and m_π=[π selected]⊕u_t. The selection is empty only at 0. The reflection count equals the number of colour changes around the cycle, so it is even.
45|- **Step 4.** By the product formula (P erratum v2, item 2), the prefix reflection parity before gap t or before f_t is ≡u_t, so the sign is c_t=(−1)^{u_t}. I checked three identities:
46|  - (c_t−c_{t+1})/2 = u_{t+1}−u_t in all four cases.
47|  - Summation by parts works with u_1=u_{F+1}=0.
48|  - c_t[m⊕u_t] = m−u_t for u_t∈{0,1}.
49|
50|  Together these give V=Σ_{t≥2}β_t u_t+Σx_π m_π. V has degree ≤1 and may vanish identically (P erratum v1).
51|- **Step 5.** Z agrees with |S| on the box, and deg Z≤2. So deg Q≤3p−3<3p−2≤M. A monomial of degree <M omits some variable, and flipping that variable pairs terms of opposite sign. The point x=0 contributes +1, so a nonzero solution exists.
52|- **Step 6.** The selection is nonempty, has even size ≤4p−1, and its size is ≡0 mod p. So the size is exactly 2p.
53|- **Order.** Write the original word as AB and the rotated word as BA. Then P_A P_B = P_B^{−1}(P_B P_A)P_B, so the same positions give the identity in the original order (P erratum v2, item 1).
54|
55|**Effect of the overlays.** The degree wording, product formula, original-order and notation overlays are exposition only. HK uses only the contrapositive of P's statement, so none of them can affect an HK step.
56|
57|**Trust.** P is a written theorem, accepted at 2026-10-21T04:22:45 by both families and checked by a parent derivation. No computation and no unproved extension is a premise.
58|
59|The header (LF1–7: brute-force checks, "NOT yet independently reviewed") lies outside the accepted mathematical text LF8–93. So do the Remarks (LF95–109: finite data, composite sharpness, conditional Brink extension). Both are historical, not re-executed, and not used by HK.
60|
61|**Not backdated.** The HK manuscript postdates cp-04 (14:52:16), so its use of P falls within the gate's forward scope. Verdict LF13's non-backdating sentence concerns K0T/LOWER-K1, not HK.

111|## 3. Re-derivation record (condensed)
112|
113|**General NF and GRX.** Every ℓ_i is odd, so δ=k−Φ and C_max=2H+Φ. Substituting n=2p−C into GRX LF18–19 gives (G) exactly. Because Σθ_i=0, an interval P=R_{j+1..j′} has T=y_j′−y_j. If H≥p, the all-plus pattern would give an identity, so κ̂≥0.
114|
115|**Critical cell (p≥5, k=p+2, κ̂=1).**
116|- H=p−2, F=3p−2 and R=p.
117|- There are p maximal cyclic reflection runs. A ρ_0 is a rotation letter, not an empty gap.
118|- All-plus (G) forbids zero-sums of even length in [4,p], and every nonempty zero-sum of odd terms has even length.
119|- So Y is either zero-sum-free or has a zero-sum pair. The parity of |Y| plays no role (M-1).
120|
121|**Padding.**
122|- In each block, reduce the selected count c to c mod 2; this is valid because σ_y^{c+2j}=σ_y^c.
123|- For c∈{0,1}, block i can take up to h_i extra pairs, and any total from 0 to H is realisable.
124|- So u has an identity 2p-subword exactly when the skeleton has an identity selection of even length L∈[2κ̂+2,2p].
125|- Arbitrary h_i are kept throughout; GRX sees the blocks only through 2H+Φ.
126|
127|**COLOR.**
128|- σ_aσ_bσ_cσ_d=ρ_{a−b+c−d}, so two non-interlaced doubled labels give an identity of length 4.
129|- Three pairwise-interlaced doubled labels must sit as ABCABC, whose product is (σ_Aσ_Bσ_C)²=1.
130|- The count p+2 = (#labels present) + (#doubled labels) then forces exactly two doubles and all p labels present.
131|- C=2A−B differs from A and B and occurs once. The A-to-A arc that avoids C contains a B, and the selection ABAC has product ρ_{2A−B−C}=1.
132|- Padding needs (2p−4)/2≤H and (2p−6)/2≤H, which hold for p≥5.
133|
134|The hypothesis is on **block labels**, as parent E1 states. A bound on raw reflection-letter multiplicity would contradict arbitrary h_i.
135|
136|The hypothesis also cannot simply be dropped. cp-02 LF60–62 gives the representative sequence 0,0,0,1,…,p−1. That note is not a premise, but I rechecked it by hand: the sequence has no pure-reflection identity of even length ≥4, because alternating sums of a weakly increasing sequence lie in (−p,0]. This is why §3 has to handle a label occurring three or more times through rotations.
137|
138|**§3 (Y has a zero-sum pair).**
139|- **x=0:** since k>p, some block label repeats. The Φ=2, T=0 interval pattern together with the two zero letters gives n=2∈[2,p].
140|- **x≠0, some label occurs ≥3 times:** three occurrences cut the cycle into three arcs, and one arc misses both marked letters. Taking P to be that arc, both letters get sign +1 and cancel.
141|- **Otherwise:** every label occurs at most twice, and COLOR plus padding applies.
142|- No S(p) is used in §3.
143|
144|**§4, coefficients.** Parity is a homomorphism Z_{2p}→Z_2, so every a_i is odd (M-3). Put E=W−p≤p−1.
145|- s≥2: b≤E−2(s−1)+1≤p−2s+2≤p−s=u_one.
146|- s=1: b≤p. The case b=p is exactly (X); otherwise b≤p−2.
147|- s=0: every coefficient is 1.
148|
149|The signed cancellations are either one coefficient-1 term on each side (size 2), or b against b coefficient-1 terms (size b+1, which is even and ≤p−1). Distinct occurrences are used throughout.
150|
151|**§4, palettes.** Suppose equal labels sit in different maximal runs. Then both arcs between them contain rotations, T=0, and the cancellation size lies in [2,p−1]⊂[2,p]. So the p palettes are disjoint. There are p+2 blocks in p runs, and the empty-run rule forces some palette to have size ≥2. That would need at least p+1 colours, a contradiction.
152|
153|**(X).**
154|- g=(1,c) with c≠0, and pg=(1,0), so the rotations are ρ_c^{p−1}ρ_0.
155|- Take two nonadjacent runs as the minus set; this gives Φ=4 and exists because k≥7.
156|- For each d∈[−B,A], select |d| signed c-letters, adding ρ_0 when |d| is odd. The size is even and ≤p−1, and the values dc cover F_p.
157|- At Φ=4, (G) allows n∈[0,min(p,2p−4)]=[0,p] for p≥5.
158|
159|S(p) is used to *reach* (X). The deduction from (X)'s literal rotations is pure GRX.
160|
161|**p=5.**
162|- NF (stated for p≥5), GRX and P (any odd prime) all apply.
163|- S(5) is the n=10 case of S(p) erratum M-1/M-2.
164|- Padding needs 3≤H=3 and 2≤3.
165|- Bipartition sizes are ≤4.
166|- The k=7 cycle has nonadjacent runs.
167|
168|**Top family.** Here H=0, F=k, R=3p−4, Φ≤p+1 and n=2p−Φ.
169|- CT applies with κ=p−1, n_CT=p−4≥1 and |Y|−n_CT=2p, giving a capped sum ≤2p−6. This is the only (R3) test in this family.
170|- Lemma M (re-proved inline in HK) with q=p−1 needs μ≤2p−3. For Φ≥4, n∈[p−1,2p−4], so the fillers needed, n−p+1, lie in [0,p−3], within the p−2 available. Cauchy–Davenport on p−1 nonzero differences covers F_p. Hence μ≥2p−2.
171|- The heavy value is unique, since 4p−4>R. Its unsigned class is common to all Φ≥4 patterns, and such patterns exist.
172|- The class is nonzero. Otherwise 2p−2 zero letters plus a repeated label would give a Φ=2 pattern with n=2p−2 and T=0.
173|- So at most p−2 letters lie outside the class.
174|- At Φ=2 the method needs p−1 fillers but only p−2 remain. HK correctly treats this as a failed step, not a no-go.
175|
176|**HIGH-K-RES.**
177|- κ̂≤p−1 follows from H≥0.
178|- κ̂≥1 follows from P.
179|- κ̂≠1 follows from HK1, and therefore depends on S(p).
180|- k≤p+2κ̂, and R≥p follows.
181|- (R1)–(R3) are literal. The (R3) domain is max(1,2κ̂−k)≤q≤2p−k−2 with q≡k mod 2. When it is empty, that k is not removed.
182|- Every surviving NF satisfies (R0)–(R3), so the residual is **necessary**. Nothing beyond that is credited.
183|

5|## Shared antecedent and new provider

112|For \(\mu\) also to be missing requires \(d=\pm1\), contrary to the definition of a deviant. The contradiction is sound, including for a zero-valued deviant. It concerns only the stated balanced Phi2 patterns.
113|
114|The **TOP failed step** is correctly unresolved. W translates avoidance for the applicable patterns into missing-target conditions. The CD lower bound alone need not force \(\Sigma(W_P)=\mathbf F_p\), so it cannot finish the cover. It also does not prove that an actual \(W_P\) has a missing residue. Coupling the targets \(T(P)\), labels and genuine empty gaps across patterns remains unproved.
115|
116|For **LOW**, \(R=p+2\) is odd. At \(6\le\Phi\le p-1\), the window indeed contains every possible even rotation-selection size. Hence the stated even-subset-sum reformulation is exact. The pure-reflection padding window starts at six, and size two lies below the Phi2 window. HK1’s COLOR proof requiring forbidden lengths four **and** six therefore cannot simply be reused. Through the errata, “length four allowed” means only “not forbidden by this padding window”; it asserts no occurrence or feasibility inside an avoiding NF.
117|
118|On the **diagonal**, \(P_{\rm gap}=0\) forces every rotation gap length to be zero or one. Exactly \(p\) are nonempty and \(2\kappa\) are empty among the \(k\) NF gaps. Theorem P’s colors concern individual reflection occurrences, so its parameter count is \(F-1\), not \(k-1\):
119|\[
120|M=3p-3=3(p-1).
121|\]
122|Its general degree ceiling therefore does not establish the strict inequality required by its box lemma. “One parameter short” correctly describes this sufficient degree budget; it does not establish that every specialized polynomial has degree exactly \(M\).
123|
124|The attempted singleton rule \(e\mathbin{\mathrm{XOR}}u_t\) contributes \(x(e-u_t)\) to the rotation coordinate on the Boolean box, but contributes \(e+u_t\) to size parity. An independent \(e\) consequently destroys identically even size. This verifies the specific failure, without classifying other encodings.
125|
126|For the ordinary attempted cross-gap pairing, a common mark selecting both singleton rotations contributes
127|\[
128|e\big((1-2u_s)x_s+(1-2u_t)x_t\big).
129|\]
130|Only a degree-at-most-two bound is guaranteed. With \((p-1)/2\) added marks, the stated \(M=(7p-7)/2\) falls below the sufficient \(4p-3\) budget. Special cancellation and other parameterizations remain unanalyzed. Neither failure is a no-go theorem.
131|
132|**The entire residual remains unchanged.**

46|## Verification record
47|
48|**1. (G) in P-form.**
49|- All blocks are odd, so GRX's δ = #{f_i = 0} = k−Φ and C_max = 2H+Φ.
50|- Hence n = 2p−C is even and lies in [max(0, 2κ̂+2−Φ), min(R, 2p−Φ)], which is HK1 (G).
51|- T(P) = Σ_{i∈P} τ_i = Σ_{i∉P} θ_i, using Σθ = 0. An interval {j+1..j′} telescopes to y_{j′}−y_j.
52|- Erratum 1 sets m = Φ = 0 for both P = ∅ and P = all. This makes the complement identities hold for every P. No proof reads P = all with Φ ≠ 0.
53|
54|**2. (D).**
55|- F = 3p−2−δ, R = p+δ, Pgap ≤ δ, and 0 ≤ δ ≤ 2κ̂−2.
56|- At δ = 0 every r_i ≤ 1 and Σr_i = p. So there are p singleton runs and 2κ̂ genuinely empty runs.
57|
58|**3. Z1.**
59|- Since k > p, pigeonhole gives y_j = y_{j′} with j ≠ j′.
60|- P = {j+1..j′} has size in [1, k−1], so it is proper and nonempty. Φ = 2 and T = 0.
61|- The window is [2κ̂, min(R, 2p−2)].
62|- n = 2κ̂ needs 2κ̂ ≤ z0 ≤ R and 2κ̂ ≤ 2p−2; both hold. Signed zeros are zero in either zone.
63|
64|**4. Z2.**
65|- If z0 ∈ [1, δ+1], there are at least p−1 nonzero letters.
66|- 2κ̂+2 ≤ p+1 < k change blocks are available. An even number of changes gives a consistent cyclic z.
67|- The window [0, min(p+δ, 2p−2κ̂−2)] contains every even n ≤ p−1.
68|- p−1 two-element sets {0, s_i} give all of Z_p by iterated Cauchy–Davenport.
69|- If |I| is odd then |I| ≤ p−2, and an unused zero letter exists to fix parity.
70|- I = ∅ gives n = 0 with C = 2p = C_max, which is admissible.
71|- Corollaries:
72|  - k = p+2 gives δ = 2κ̂−2, so [δ+2, 2κ̂−1] is empty.
73|  - Every LOW-CORE NF has κ̂ = 2 ≤ (p−1)/2.
74|  - On the diagonal with κ̂ ≥ (p+1)/2, Z1 is vacuous because 2κ̂−1 ≥ p = R. The table understates nothing.
75|
76|**5. TOP setup and (H).**
77|- C_max = Φ, so n = 2p−Φ. k is odd, so m ≤ (p+1)/2 and n ∈ [p−1, 2p] ⊆ [0, R].
78|- (v,e) ↦ (λv, e) is an automorphism. It preserves letter order, blocks, the empty-gap rule and zero letters, and sends T ↦ λT and X^P ↦ λX^P. It is applied to u only.
79|- The HK1 item-3 import is re-checked:
80|  - The pair lemma at q = p−1 needs μ ≤ 2p−3.
81|  - The p−2 spare letters cover the at most p−3 fillers.
82|  - The heavy value is unique since 2(2p−2) > 3p−4, and its class is common across patterns.
83|  - The class is nonzero by the Φ2, T0 pattern at n = 2p−2.
84|- Signed ±1 multiplicities are exactly S_±, because a signed deviant (including value 0) is never ±1.
85|- With S_+ + S_− = 3p−4−D, (H) is equivalent to max(S_±) ≥ 2p−2.
86|
87|**6. Lemma W.**
88|- For 2 ≤ Φ ≤ p+1: p−1 ≤ n ≤ 2p−2 ≤ S_+, and there are at most p−2 non-unit letters, so fewer than n.
89|- Every set V of non-unit letters completes with signed ones, and every n-selection splits this way. So Σ_n = n + Σ(W_P), and −n ≡ Φ.
90|
91|**7. BIG-RUN.** Checked vertex by vertex.
92|- Growth phase: every X with {1,5} ⊆ X ⊆ all∖{2,6} has distinct complement pieces through 2 (inside [2,4]) and through 6 (inside [6,N]). So m ≥ 2.
93|- The complements of Xb..Xe are {1,2}∪{6}, {1}∪{6}, {1}∪{5,6} and {1}∪{5}. These pieces are separated whenever N ≥ 7, and here N = p+2 ≥ 9.
94|- The path is N−4 growth flips followed by four flips (−1, +2, −5, +6).
95|- Δ(X^c) = −Δ(X), and |Δ| ≥ p+D > 0 in the integers.
96|- Flipping run q changes Δ by ∓2g_q, so |g_q| ≥ p+D.
97|
98|**8. (AP) and SIDE.**
99|- The all-plus pattern has n = 2p and T = 0.
100|  - A ≥ 2p would give 2p ones summing to zero.
101|  - A′ ≥ p, together with A ≥ p, would give p ones plus p minus-ones.
102|- If q ∉ P, then S_− ≤ A′+O ≤ 2p−4−2D < 2p−2. (H) then gives S_+ ≥ 2p−2 and S_− ≤ p−2−D.
103|- The q ∈ P case follows by complement. |W_P| = S_− + D ≤ p−2.
104|
105|**9. BALANCE.**
106|- For every e ≤ D, S_± ≤ 2p−2−D ≤ n−e. So i ranges over [n−e−S_−, S_+], a run of N_e = p−1−D+e values, with N_e in [1, p−1].
107|- The signed sums form a step-2 progression of N_e < p distinct residues.
108|- With e = D, the only residue missed is μ.
109|- Dropping one deviant d (e = D−1), the residues missed are μ−d±1.
110|- Avoidance would need d ≡ ±1, which is impossible for a deviant, including d = 0.
111|- When D = 0, μ ≡ 2S_+ + 4 ≡ −2S_− − 4, which agrees with cp02 LF19.
112|
113|**10. LOW-CORE.**
114|- For 6 ≤ Φ ≤ p−1 the window is every even n ≤ p+1, since R = p+2 is odd. So the window is exactly E(X^P).
115|- The Φ2 window is [4, p+1], so HK1's n = 2 step is unavailable.
116|- The pure skeleton window is [6, 2p]. ABCABC (length 6) stays forbidden; length-4 selections are only not forbidden.
117|- Both stated failure points are accurate.
118|
119|**11. Diagonal.**
120|- Every Theorem P gap has floor(n_t/2) = 0, so M = 3p−3 = 3(p−1). That only matches the degree bound, and the box lemma needs strict inequality.
121|- The four listed single-letter rules are confirmed:
122|  - "select iff u_t" contributes −x·u_t.
123|  - "select iff 1−u_t" contributes (1−u_t)·x.
124|  - "never" contributes nothing.
125|  - "select iff e⊕u_t" contributes (e−u_t)·x and adds e+u_t to the size parity.
126|- Even with all p singletons, M = 4p−3 exceeds the degree bound. The obstruction is parity, as stated.
127|
128|**12. Residual.**
129|- Every auxiliary statement is the contrapositive of an explicit (R2) violation on (R0) data. Adjoining them leaves the (R0)–(R3) set unchanged.
130|- No exclusion, feasibility claim or converse is made.
131|
132|## Findings and minimal new errata
133|
134|**R-1 — MINOR, required documentary (cp03 LF14).**
135|- Problem:
136|  - cp03 says "W-formulation valid with UNRESTRICTED subset sums whenever S_+≥2p−2 and Φ≤p+1". It omits Φ ≥ 2.
137|  - At Φ = 0 (n = 2p) with S_+ ∈ {2p−2, 2p−1}, every 2p-selection needs at least 2p−S_+ ≥ 1 non-unit letters. V = ∅ is unavailable, so Σ_{2p} = 2p + Σ(W_P) fails in general.
138|  - The parenthetical checks only the non-unit side.
139|  - The manuscript's Lemma W (proof LF96) already carries Φ ≥ 2.
140|- Erratum: *At cp03 LF14 read "whenever 2≤Φ≤p+1 and S_+≥2p−2 (so n=2p−Φ≤2p−2≤S_+ and the non-unit part is ≤p−2<n)". No lemma or use changes.*
141|
142|**R-2 — MINOR, required documentary (proof LF143).**
143|- Problem:
144|  - "|Σ(W_P)|≥|W_P|+1≤p−1, so no single pattern forces an identity" chains inequalities in mixed directions.
145|  - It also reads as an absolute claim. For given data, a single pattern does give an identity whenever T(P)+2m(P) ∈ Σ(W_P).
146|- Erratum: *Read "Cauchy–Davenport guarantees only |Σ(W_P)| ≥ |W_P|+1, and |W_P|+1 ≤ p−1. So this size bound alone does not force T(P)+2m(P) ∈ Σ(W_P) for any single pattern. Nothing is asserted about whether an actual configuration avoids a pattern."*
147|
148|**R-3 — MINOR, required documentary.**
149|- Locations: proof LF164–165, E1 LF17–18, parent erratum LF5 (item 1, second sentence), cp01 LF23–24, package v2 LF25.
150|- Problem: the "attempted cross-gap encoding" is never written down, yet parent item 1 asserts a guaranteed degree-≤2 bound for it.
151|- That bound holds for the plain-mark rule: for x in gap t and x′ in gap t′≠t, select both iff m_π.
152|  - V gains m_π((1−2u_t)x + (1−2u_{t′})x′).
153|  - Z gains 2m_π, so parity, injectivity and the zero point are kept.
154|  - deg Q ≤ 4(p−1), and M = (7p−7)/2 ≤ 4(p−1).
155|- The bound fails for a Theorem-P-style rule "select iff m_π⊕u_t": the multilinear form of V then contains 4x′u_{t′}m_πu_t, which is degree 3 when x′ ≠ 0.
156|- The method fails under either rule, so nothing load-bearing is affected.
157|- Erratum: *The attempted encoding is the plain-mark rule (worker to confirm). For it, deg V ≤ 2, deg Q ≤ 4(p−1) and M = (7p−7)/2 < 4p−3. If an XOR-with-u_t rule was used instead, read "degree ≤ 3", which also fails. No classification and no no-go theorem.*
158|
159|**O-1 — MINOR, optional (proof LF6–8, package v2 LF20, cp03 LF16–17).**
160|- Problem:
161|  - "Because GRX is exact … unchanged", "GRX is exact, so the residual set is unchanged" and "GRX is an IFF, so …" give the wrong reason.
162|  - The real reason is that each auxiliary statement is a formal consequence of (R0)+(R2). Exactness and the converse direction are unused and promote nothing.
163|  - "Data subclasses of HIGH-K-RES" should read "data subclasses of the (R0)–(R1) data space, which meet HIGH-K-RES trivially".

167|- This is the scalar consequence of the same accepted P-fail inequality, not a new use. Suggested reading: "through HK1's (R0) k-range and (R1) gap floor".

30|THEOREM D1-UE (candidate). For every prime p>=7 and kappa in {p-3,p-2,p-1} with (p,kappa)!=(7,4), every word of
31|C(p,kappa) has an identity2p-subword.
32|THEOREM D1-MP (candidate). For every prime p>=7, every word of C(p,(p+1)/2) has an identity2p-subword.
33|COROLLARY (union with the accepted baseline, Sec.6). DELTA1-PAIR-COVER holds for every prime p>=19 and every
34|kappa in [2,p-1]; for p in {7,11,13,17} it holds for every kappa except kappa=(p-1)/2.

36|## 1. Normalisation and avoider data
37|
38|The automorphism (v,eps)->(c^(-1)v,eps) of D_(2p) maps rho_(+-c) to rho_(+-1), preserves letter order, block lengths,
39|maximality, empty runs and identity selections; normalisation N (v->-v, K0star-r2 LF35-36) gives A>=B. So u is in
40|setting (S): R=p+1>=1, F=4p-2-R=3p-3>=1. Write u=R_1S_1...R_kS_k (run R_t precedes block S_t), S_t of odd length
41|2h_t+1, H=sum h_t=p-1-kappa, F=2H+k, hence k=p-1+2kappa. Assume u avoids.
42|(A1) By (P0) with e_F=0, at most 2kappa runs R_t have an even number of letters (empty runs count as even), so
43|o_R>=k-2kappa=p-1 runs are odd, hence nonempty.
44|(A2) sum_t r_t=p+1 and r_t=[R_t odd] mod2 give o_R even, so o_R in{p-1,p+1}. The excess
45|sum_(odd runs)(r_t-1)+sum_(even nonempty runs)r_t=p+1-o_R is 0 or 2, each odd-run term even>=0, each even-nonempty
46|term even>=2. Exactly one case holds: (Q+) o_R=p+1, every nonempty run has one letter, q=p+1; (Q0) one nonempty
47|run has 2 letters, all others one, q=p; (Q-) one run has 3 letters, all others one, q=p-1. Here q is the number of
48|nonempty runs N_1..N_q (cyclic order). Hence d:=max_j|d(N_j)|<=3, and d=1 in case (Q+).
49|(A3) M_i is the maximal reflection run between N_i and N_(i+1) (i=1..q), |M_i|>=1 blocks, sum_i|M_i|=k; consecutive
50|blocks of M_i are separated by EMPTY runs, which are genuine. A pair is a two-block arc of two consecutive blocks of
51|one M_i (nu=2); a singleton is a one-block arc (nu=1). Pair capacity c:=sum_i floor(|M_i|/2)=(k-o_M)/2 with
52|o_M:=#{i:|M_i| odd}<=q and o_M=k mod2. A block of M_i lying in no pair is FREE (it may be a singleton or unused).
53|(A4) For every arc choice (v5 Sec.3, sum c_i even, >=2), T=sum_j z_j d(N_j) is EVEN, since d(N_j)=r(N_j) mod2 and
54|sum_j r(N_j)=p+1. By (BAL) with I.6, for every index i there is i'!=i such that changing the parities of c_i and
55|c_(i') gives |T|<=d; then |T|<=2, and T=0 in case (Q+). If already |T|<=d nothing is changed.
56|
57|## 2. Lemma GW (direct-window arc lemma; fresh proof)
58|
59|Let u be in (S). Let an arc choice consist of 2m>=2 pairwise disjoint arcs, each a singleton or a pair, G of them
60|pairs, with Lemma-3.1 value T, and put E_+-=(R+-T)/2. If gamma_m(E_+)>=0, gamma_m(E_-)>=0 and
61|   gamma_m(E_+)+gamma_m(E_-)+G>=p-1,                                                         (GW)
62|gamma_m(E):=min(p-m,E-kappa+m-1,floor(E/2)) as in (W), then u has an identity2p-subword.
63|Proof. Number the arcs cyclically X_1,Y_1,...,X_m,Y_m; each gap contains the run right after its arc, so this is an
64|arc system. By (L2.1), for every a_i in X_i and b_i in Y_i, P:=union_i of the runs R_(a_i+1),...,R_(b_i) is a
65|proper pattern with exactly m intervals, E+(P)=E+ (the base value, independent of the choices) and
66|u_P=(1/2)sum_i(y_(a_i)-y_(b_i)). By (L3.1), {E+(P),E-(P)}={E_+,E_-}. The window of (W),
67|I=[-gamma_m(E-(P)),gamma_m(E+(P))], is an integer interval containing 0 with gamma_m(E_+)+gamma_m(E_-)+1 integers,
68|hence min(p,gamma_m(E_+)+gamma_m(E_-)+1) residues. The set of attained u_P contains the sumset of the sets
69|(1/2)V(X_i) and -(1/2)V(Y_i), V(X) the set of block values of X (|V|=1 for a singleton, 2 for a pair, since the run
70|inside a pair is a genuine empty run); (CD) gives at least min(p,1+G) residues. If either count is p the two sets
71|meet; otherwise the counts sum to >=p+1 by (GW), so they meet. Some choice has u_P congruent to an element of I, and
72|(W) gives an identity2p-subword (cyclic, hence in increasing original order by conjugation). []
73|With s:=p-m one has E-kappa+m-1=E+H-s, so gamma_m(E)=min(s,E-s+H,floor(E/2)). Put e:=(p+1)/2, so E_+-=e+-T/2, and
74|Gamma(s,T):=gamma(E_+)+gamma(E_-).
75|
76|## 3. Theorem D1-UE (kappa=p-1-H, H in {0,1,2})
77|
78|Here k=3p-3-2H. Put s0:=floor((p+1)/4), m0:=p-s0.
79|Step 1 (window). For T in{0,+2,-2} and every H>=0: Gamma(s0,T)>=e-1=(p-1)/2, both gamma>=0.
80| p=4a+1 (a>=3), e=2a+1, s0=a. T=0: gamma(2a+1)=min(a,a+1+H,a)=a; Gamma=2a. |T|=2: gamma(2a+2)=min(a,a+2+H,a+1)=a,
81| gamma(2a)=min(a,a+H,a)=a; Gamma=2a=e-1.
82| p=4a+3 (a>=1), e=2a+2, s0=a+1. T=0: gamma(2a+2)=min(a+1,a+1+H,a+1)=a+1; Gamma=2a+2=e. |T|=2:
83| gamma(2a+3)=min(a+1,a+2+H,a+1)=a+1, gamma(2a+1)=min(a+1,a+H,a)=a; Gamma=2a+1=e-1.
84|So by Lemma GW any singleton/pair choice with exactly 2m0 arcs, at least (p-1)/2 pairs and |T|<=2 gives an identity.
85|Step 2 (counts). c>=(k-q)/2>=(k-p-1)/2=p-2-H. G0:=(p+1)/2. Since 2s0=(p-1)/2 (p=4a+1) or (p+1)/2 (p=4a+3),
86|G0+2m0=2p+1 resp. 2p, and 2m0-G0=p resp. p-1, which is >=1.
87|Step 3 (initial choice with a designated run M_l). Exactly 2m0 arcs, exactly G0 pairs, and a run M_l carrying a
88|designated singleton and either (type A) a designated UNUSED block or (type B) a designated pair.
89| Type A, when c>=G0+1. Choose l with |M_l|>=2 (k-q>=2p-4-2H>0). Designate the first block of M_l as the singleton and
90| the second as the reserved unused block; the other |M_l|-2 consecutive blocks still hold floor(|M_l|/2)-1 disjoint
91| pairs, so c-1>=G0 pairs avoid the two designated blocks; take G0 of them. The 2m0-G0-1 further singletons go on
92| unused blocks other than the reserved one; this needs G0+2m0<=k-1.
93| c>=G0+1 holds if p-2-H>=(p+3)/2, i.e. H<=(p-7)/2: every p>=11 (H<=2) and p=7,H=0. G0+2m0<=k-1 holds for p>=11 since
94| G0+2m0<=2p+1<=3p-8<=k-1, and for p=7 (p=4a+3, G0+2m0=14) when H<=1 (k-1=17-2H).
95| Type B, the only other case with H<=2 and (p,H)!=(7,2): p=7, H=1, c=4=G0. Then k=16, c=(16-o_M)/2 forces o_M=8, so
96| q=8 and every |M_i| is odd; as sum|M_i|=16>8, some |M_l|>=3. In M_l designate blocks1-2 as a pair and block3 as the
97| singleton, add (|M_l|-3)/2 further pairs, and floor(|M_i|/2) pairs in every other M_i: G0=4 pairs. The other 5 of the
98| 6=2m0-G0 singletons go on free blocks of other runs (7 available). Blocks used: G0+2m0=14<=16.
99|Step 4 (balancing at fixed arc count). Apply (BAL) at i=l; let i'!=l. Realise the parity change at i' by an
100|applicable e5/I.7 move: delete a singleton (eps'=-1), add a singleton on an unused block (eps'=+1), or split a pair
101|(eps'=+1, one pair lost). Realise the change at l by: eps'=+1: delete the designated singleton; eps'=-1: add a
102|singleton on the reserved block (type A, no loss) or split the designated pair (type B, one pair lost). Each is a
103|valid parity change (I.7). The arc count stays 2m0; a pair is lost at i' only when eps'=+1, and then the move at l is
104|a deletion; a pair is lost at l only when eps'=-1, and then the move at i' is a deletion. So at most ONE pair is
105|lost, G>=(p-1)/2, the result consists of singletons and pairs, and |T|<=2 by (A4).
106|Step 5. Lemma GW with m=m0 (Step 1) gives an identity2p-subword, contradicting avoidance. []
107|
108|## 4. Theorem D1-MP (kappa=e=(p+1)/2, H=e-2, k=2p)
109|
110|Step 1 (window range; gamma(E)=min(s,E-s+e-2,floor(E/2))).
111| p=4a+1 (a>=3, e=2a+1, H=2a-1). T=0: gamma(2a+1)=min(s,4a-s,a)=a for a<=s<=3a; Gamma=2a. |T|=2:
112| gamma(2a+2)=min(s,4a+1-s,a+1)>=a and gamma(2a)=min(s,4a-1-s,a)=a for a<=s<=3a-1; at s=3a, gamma(2a+2)=a+1 and
113| gamma(2a)=a-1. So Gamma(s,T)>=2a for every s in[a,3a] and T in{0,+-2}; all gamma>=a-1>=0. (GW) needs G>=4a-2a=2a.
114| p=4a+3 (a>=1, e=2a+2, H=2a). T=0: gamma(2a+2)=min(s,4a+2-s,a+1)=a+1 for a+1<=s<=3a+1; Gamma=2a+2. |T|=2:
115| gamma(2a+3)=min(s,4a+3-s,a+1)=a+1 and gamma(2a+1)=min(s,4a+1-s,a)=a for a+1<=s<=3a+1; Gamma=2a+1. (GW) needs
116| G>=2a if T=0 and G>=2a+1=(p-1)/2 if |T|=2.
117|Step 2 (initial choice). s0:=a+1 (p=4a+1) or a+2 (p=4a+3), m0:=p-s0; then s0-1,s0,s0+1 lie in the Step-1 range
118|(a>=1). Choose G0 pairs:
119| (Q0),(Q-): o_M<=q<=p and o_M even give o_M<=p-1, c>=(p+1)/2; G0:=(p+1)/2.
120| (Q+): o_M<=p+1 gives c>=(p-1)/2. If c>=(p+1)/2, G0:=(p+1)/2. If c=(p-1)/2, then o_M=p+1=q, every |M_i| is odd;
121| take G0:=c, i.e. floor(|M_i|/2) pairs in every M_i, so every M_i has exactly ONE free block.
122| Blocks: 2m0+G0<=k=2p iff G0<=2s0, and 2s0=(p+3)/2 resp. (p+5)/2>=(p+1)/2. Then 2m0-G0 singletons fit on the
123| 2p-2G0 free blocks, and 2m0-G0>=0. Since 2G0<2p, some M_i has a free block; fix such i.
124|Step 3 (balancing with flexible arc count). Apply (BAL) at this i; let i'!=i. Realise both parity changes by the e5
125|rule. At i it deletes a singleton or adds one on an unused block (M_i has a free block), never splitting; at i' it
126|splits a pair only if M_(i') has no free block, never in the case c=(p-1)/2 of (Q+). The arc count becomes
127|2m0+Delta, Delta in{-2,0,2}, so s'=s0-Delta/2 in{s0-1,s0,s0+1} lies in the Step-1 range and the new m'>=m0-1>=1;
128|the result consists of singletons and pairs; G>=G0-1, with G=G0 in the case c=(p-1)/2; |T|<=2 and T=0 in (Q+).
129|Step 4. (Q0),(Q-): G>=(p-1)/2 suffices for both residue classes. (Q+),p=4a+3: T=0 and G>=G0-1>=(p-3)/2=2a.
130|(Q+),p=4a+1: G>=(p-1)/2=2a (G0=(p+1)/2 with one possible loss, or G0=(p-1)/2 with none). Lemma GW at s' gives an
131|identity2p-subword, contradicting avoidance. []
132|Check p=7 (a=1): s0=3, s' in{2,3,4}; Gamma=4 (T=0) or 3 (|T|=2); (Q+) G>=2, (Q0),(Q-) G>=3; blocks 2m0+G0<=12<=14.
133|
134|## 5. Why kappa=(p-1)/2 at p in{7,11,13,17} is NOT closed here (method failure, not a no-go)
135|
136|At kappa=(p-1)/2: H=e-1, k=2p-2 and c=(2p-2-o_M)/2. For p=4a+1 (p=13,17), case (Q+) with every |M_i| odd gives
137|c=(p-3)/2=2a-1, while Gamma(s,0)<=2floor(e/2)=2a for every s, so Gamma+G<=4a-1<p-1: the pair-only route of Lemma GW
138|is one short. For p=4a+3 (p=7,11), case (Q0) with a pure two-letter run, o_M=p-1 and the unique even |M_j| fully
139|paired, c=(p-1)/2 equals the requirement for |T|=2, and the partner index of (BAL) may be that fully paired run,
140|losing a pair; the construction above does not close this sub-case. Longer arcs, segments (ARC+), raw SEG, B.4 or a
141|refined balancing might close some cells; none is claimed. No avoiding word is asserted.
142|
143|## 6. Union with the accepted baseline; ENTIRE residual; diagnostics
144|
145|Accepted numerical providers at delta=1 (universe kappa in[2,p-1]), by hand set algebra (cp-01):
146|RPdelta E1: A(p,1)=[ceil((p+5)/4),(p-5)/2] u [(p+3)/2,p-4], E(p,1)=empty; B-prime N(p,1): (p-1)/2 iff p>=19,
147|(p-3)/2 iff p>=17. RP-2NE: [2,(p-5)/2]. D1-END: {(p-3)/2}. New (candidate): {(p+1)/2} u {p-3,p-2,p-1}, all p>=7.
148|Union: p>=19: all of [2,p-1]. p in{11,13,17}: [2,p-1] minus {(p-1)/2}. p=7: {2,4,5,6}.
149|ENTIRE remaining residual of DELTA1-PAIR-COVER: words of C(p,(p-1)/2) for p in{7,11,13,17}, i.e. the four cells
150|(p,kappa)=(7,3),(11,5),(13,6),(17,8) at R=p+1, any B, with all target word data. Accepted necessary/word-dependent
151|conditions there (not universal bands, no feasibility): (A1)-(A4); failure of raw Theorem SEG's hypotheses (v5 with
152|errata4/5); at (17,8) RPdelta E5/B.4: |AB|<=N_O and x=0; the B=0 CJ/EDGE coverage recorded in the RPdelta verdict
153|(not read or bound here, not used). FIRST UNPROVED LEMMA D1-SMALL-MIDDLE: no word of C(p,(p-1)/2) avoids, for
154|p in{7,11,13,17}.

33| e=(p+1)/2, kappa=e-1, H=e-1, k=2p-2; N_1,...,N_q, M_1,...,M_q, b_i=|M_i|, d_i=d(N_i),

40|## 1. The exact window ranges
41|
42|For p=4a+1, a>=3 (p>=7 prime), e=2a+1, H=2a:
43| T=0: gamma(e)=min(s,4a+1-s,a)=a for a<=s<=3a+1, so Gamma=2a.
44| |T|=2: gamma(e+1)=min(s,4a+2-s,a+1), gamma(e-1)=min(s,4a-s,a).
45| For a+1<=s<=3a, these are a+1 and a, so Gamma=2a+1.
46| At s=a both equal a; at s=3a+1 they are a+1 and a-1. Hence for every s in[a,3a+1] and
47| T in{0,+2,-2}, both gamma>=0 and Gamma>=2a. Lemma GW therefore needs G>=2a on that larger interval,
48| or G>=2a-1 when |T|=2 and s in[a+1,3a].
49|
50|For p=4a+3, a>=1, e=2a+2, H=2a+1:
51| T=0: gamma(e)=min(s,4a+3-s,a+1)=a+1 for a+1<=s<=3a+2, so Gamma=2a+2.
52| |T|=2: gamma(e+1)=min(s,4a+4-s,a+1)=a+1 and
53| gamma(e-1)=min(s,4a+2-s,a)=a on the SAME interval. Both gamma>=0; Gamma=2a+1.
54| Thus G>=2a suffices for T=0 and G>=2a+1 suffices for |T|=2.
55|These are literal minimum calculations for the entire intervals, not sampled parameter checks.

57|## 2. Capacity and the cases with room for BAL
58|
59|k is even, so o_M is even. Q+ permits o_M<=p+1, hence c>=(p-3)/2. Q0 and Q- permit o_M<=p-1,
60|hence c>=(p-1)/2. Fully pairing a reflection run means choosing floor(b_i/2) consecutive disjoint pairs;
61|if b_i is odd it then has exactly one FREE block, while if b_i is even it has none.
62|
63|BAL construction with a one-pair reserve. Choose G0<=c disjoint pairs, then 2m0-G0 singletons on free blocks,
64|where s0=a+2 and m0=p-s0. This is possible if 0<=2m0-G0 and 2m0+G0<=k, the latter equivalent to
65|G0<=2s0-2=2a+2. At least one free block exists since 2G0<k. Fix an M_i with one. BAL changes two parities;
66|the change at i adds/deletes a singleton without pair loss. The partner move deletes a singleton, adds one
67|on an unused block, or splits a pair, losing at most one pair. Thus G>=G0-1 and the new arc count is
68|2m0+Delta, Delta in{-2,0,2}; s' in[a+1,a+3]. If already |T|<=d no change is needed. Every case below has
69|m0-1>=1. T is even, |T|<=2, and T=0 in Q+. This uses the restricted e5 rule only for singletons/pairs.
70|
71|For p=4a+1: if c>=2a+1 take G0=2a+1. Blocks fit, 2m0-G0=4a-3>=0,
72|G>=2a, and [a+1,a+3] is contained in[a,3a+1]. Section1 and GW close EVERY Q-type.
73|For p=4a+3, Q+: if c>=2a+1 take G0=2a+1; 2m0-G0=4a+1>=0. Then G>=2a and T=0.
74|For p=4a+3, Q0/Q-: if c>=2a+2 take G0=2a+2; 2m0-G0=4a>=0. Then G>=2a+1.
75|In both p=4a+3 cases [a+1,a+3] is contained in[a+1,3a+2]. Thus GW closes these cases.
76|
77|All-odd tight construction. If every b_i is odd and the capacity c is one of the following values, take ALL
78|c pairs and again s0=a+2. Each M_i has one free block; the needed 2m0-c singletons fit as follows:
79| p=4a+3, Q+, c=2a: q=4a+4 free blocks; 2m0-c=4a+2.
80| p=4a+3, Q-, c=2a+1: q=4a+2 free blocks; 2m0-c=4a+1.
81| p=4a+1, Q-, c=2a: q=4a free blocks; 2m0-c=4a-2.
82|BAL changes each parity by adding/deleting its free singleton, so NO pair is lost. The same s' intervals
83|apply, and the G/T requirements of Section1 are met. These cases are forced all-odd: c at its stated value
84|gives o_M=q. Thus every case except the tight Q0 and two tight Q+ cases below is now closed.

86|## 3. Direct parity choices with rigid reflection runs
87|
88|Fix full pairing in every M_i, with g_i=floor(b_i/2). In each odd M_i choose x_i in{0,1}: its free block is
89|unused or is a singleton. Then c_i=g_i+x_i. In an even M_i put x_i=0; call it RIGID. For any z_i in{+1,-1}
90|satisfying z_(i+1)=(-1)^(g_i) z_i at every rigid edge, set x_i at every flexible edge so that
91| z_(i+1)=(-1)^(g_i+x_i) z_i.
92|This defines a valid singleton/pair choice. Around the cycle the product of these relations is1, so
93|sum_i(g_i+x_i) is even. Therefore the arc count is 2m=c+x, x=sum x_i; the corresponding T is sum z_i d_i
94|up to the immaterial global sign. The choices below have m>=1 and use at most k blocks, since all pairs
95|are disjoint and only their free blocks are used.
96|
97|Lemma DP1 (one rigid edge, Q0). For p>=7, some such z has T=0.
98|Proof. Let the rigid edge join N_j,N_(j+1). Choose their signs consistently with its fixed relation and let
99|f=z_j d_j+z_(j+1)d_(j+1). There is exactly one length-two run N_r with d_r in{-2,0,2}; all other d_i are+-1.
100|If r is one of j,j+1, then f is odd and |f|<=3. The remaining p-2 singleton contributions are freely+-1;
101|their sums are precisely the integers of parity p-2 in[-(p-2),p-2], so they can sum to -f.
102|If r is outside the pair, then f is even and |f|<=2. Choose z_r arbitrarily and put g=z_r d_r, even with
103||g|<=2. The remaining p-3 singleton contributions are freely+-1 and can sum to -(f+g), since p-3>=4,
104||f+g|<=4 and its parity is even. All unconstrained signs are free because the other edges are flexible. []
105|
106|Lemma DP2 (two rigid edges, Q+). For p=4a+1>=13, some compatible z has T=0.
107|Proof. The two rigid edges are distinct and do not form a cycle (q=p+1>=14). Their endpoint set K has
108|3 or4 vertices. Assign signs satisfying both edge relations on K; this is possible on a path or on two
109|disjoint edges. Its contribution f has |f|<=|K|<=4 and f=|K| mod2, since all d_i are+-1.
110|All other q-|K| signs are free. Their contributions fill the integers of parity q-|K| in the interval
111|[-(q-|K|),q-|K|]. Now q is even, so this parity equals |K|, and q-|K|>=p-3>=10>=|f|.
112|Choose these contributions to sum to -f. []
113|
114|Tight Q0: c=(p-1)/2 implies o_M=p-1 and q=p, so exactly ONE b_i is even. Use DP1 and full pairing.
115|For p=4a+1, c=2a is even; x is even and 0<=x<=p-1=4a. Thus a<=m<=3a,
116|so a+1<=s<=3a+1. T=0, Gamma=2a, G=c=2a, and GW holds.
117|For p=4a+3, c=2a+1 is odd; x is odd and 1<=x<=p-2=4a+1. Thus a+1<=m<=3a+1,
118|so a+2<=s<=3a+2. T=0, Gamma=2a+2, G=c=2a+1, and GW holds.
119|No BAL partner can consume a pair, since BAL is NOT used in these two tight Q0 cases.
120|
121|Tight Q+, p=4a+1, c=2a: then o_M=4a=p-1 and q=p+1, so exactly TWO b_i are even. Use DP2 and full pairing.
122|x is even and 0<=x<=p-1=4a; hence a<=m<=3a and a+1<=s<=3a+1.
123|T=0, Gamma=2a, G=2a, and GW holds.
124|
125|## 4. The all-flexible one-short Q+ case
126|
127|It remains p=4a+1, c=2a-1. Here o_M=4a+2=q=p+1, so every b_i is odd. Fully pair every M_i.
128|All edges are flexible and every sign vector z can be realised by Section3. Since every d_i is+-1,
129|choose contributions w_i=z_i d_i with exactly2a+2 of them+1 and2a of them-1. Then T=2.
130|Let F be the edge set where z changes sign, and P0={i:g_i odd}. The free-singleton set is
131|D=F symmetric-difference P0; x=|D|. Since c=2a-1 is odd and total arc count is even, x is odd,
132|so 1<=x<=q-1=4a+1. If x>=3 retain this vector.
133|If x=1, pick a vertex with w=+1. Among the2a>=6 vertices with w=-1, at most2 are its cyclic neighbours;
134|choose one that is NOT adjacent. Flip z at these two vertices. The +1 becomes-1 and the -1 becomes+1,
135|so T remains2. Their four incident edges are distinct, and F changes on exactly those four edges.
136|Consequently the new D has size3 (if its old single edge was among those four) or5 (otherwise).
137|Thus a realisation with 3<=x<=4a+1 exists; the upper bound also follows from x odd and q even.
138|Now 2m=c+x gives a+1<=m<=3a, hence a+1<=s<=3a. Section1 gives Gamma=2a+1 at |T|=2.
139|G=c=2a-1, so Gamma+G=4a=p-1. Lemma GW gives an identity2p-subword, contradiction.
140|This resolves the one-short T=0 obstruction by changing the actual target value T, not by inventing capacity.
141|

93|### Checked and consistent

95|- **Hash chain:** S2→S1, S3→S2, S6→S7 and S8, S10→S9, and S12→S6 through S11 all string-match.
96|- **Clocks:** S6's duration of 567 s and S11's gap of 261 s are correct. 16:14:57Z equals 09:14:57 PDT, and the overall chronology is in order.
97|- **Counts:** S9 has 26 locators. The Zhang–Cao exception list has exactly 17 entries.
98|- **Group facts:** orders 21 and 39 each have two groups; orders 35 and 51 have only the cyclic group.
99|- **CFR(r,n) ⇔ (Zₙ,r+1;1) difference matrix holds** under the standard definition:
100|  - Let gᵢ be the map from symbol to position in row i.
101|  - Rows i ≠ j conflict exactly when gᵢ − gⱼ is not a bijection.
102|  - Adding the row g₀ = 0 gives the difference matrix; normalizing a difference matrix by one row reverses this.
103|  - So S5's "[inference]" is right, and its CFR(4,33) caveat follows.
104|- **S8's arithmetic is consistent:**
105|  - F + R = 4p − 2, the GRX window and the δ identities;
106|  - Z1's window;
107|  - Z2's Cauchy–Davenport count, which reaches p, and its half-range κ ≤ (p−1)/2;

109|  - BALANCE at D = 0, which gives T = 2S₊ + 4 because n = 2p − 2;
110|  - M = 3p − 3, and (7p−7)/2 < 4p − 3.
